###### Definition E.18 (Support of 𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} ) .

Let 𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} be any reward function distribution. supp ⁡ ( 𝒟 any ) \optSupp(\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}) is the smallest closed subset of ℝ | 𝒮 | \mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} whose complement has measure zero under 𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} .

###### Definition E.19 (Linear functional optimality probability) .

For finite A , B ⊊ ℝ | 𝒮 | A,B\subsetneq\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} , the probability under 𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} that A A is optimal over B B is p 𝒟 any ​ ( A ≥ B ) ≔ ℙ 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 any ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ≥ max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) p_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(A\geq B\right)\coloneqq\Prb\nolimits_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\geq\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right) .

###### Proposition E.20 (Non-dominated linear functionals and their optimality probability) .

Let A ⊊ ℝ | 𝒮 | A\subsetneq\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} be finite. If ∃ b < c : [ b , c ] | 𝒮 | ⊆ supp ⁡ ( 𝒟 any ) \exists b<c\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}[b,c]^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}\subseteq\optSupp(\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}) , then 𝐚 ∈ ND ​ ( A ) \mathbf{a}\in\text{{ND}}\left(A\right) implies that 𝐚 \mathbf{a} is strictly optimal for a set of reward functions with positive measure under 𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} .

###### Proof.

Suppose ∃ b < c : [ b , c ] | 𝒮 | ⊆ supp ⁡ ( 𝒟 any ) \exists b<c\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}[b,c]^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}\subseteq\optSupp(\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}) . If 𝐚 ∈ ND ​ ( A ) \mathbf{a}\in\text{{ND}}\left(A\right) , then let 𝐫 \mathbf{r} be such that 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 > max 𝐚 ′ ∈ A ∖ { 𝐚 } ⁡ 𝐚 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝐫 \mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}>\max_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\in A\setminus\left\{\mathbf{a}\right\}}\mathbf{a}^{\prime\top}\mathbf{r} . For a 1 > 0 , a 2 ∈ ℝ a_{1}>0,a_{2}\in\mathbb{R} , positively affinely transform 𝐫 ′ ≔ a 1 ​ 𝐫 + a 2 ​ 𝟏 \mathbf{r}^{\prime}\coloneqq a_{1}\mathbf{r}+a_{2}\mathbf{1} (where 𝟏 ∈ ℝ | 𝒮 | \mathbf{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} is the all-ones vector) so that 𝐫 ′ ∈ ( b , c ) | 𝒮 | \mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in(b,c)^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} .

Note that 𝐚 \mathbf{a} is still strictly optimal for 𝐫 ′ \mathbf{r}^{\prime} : 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 > max 𝐚 ′ ∈ A ∖ { 𝐚 } ⁡ 𝐚 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ⇔ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ′ > max 𝐚 ′ ∈ A ∖ { 𝐚 } ⁡ 𝐚 ′ ⁣ ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ′ . \mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}>\max_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\in A\setminus\left\{\mathbf{a}\right\}}\mathbf{a}^{\prime\top}\mathbf{r}\iff\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{\prime}>\max_{\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\in A\setminus\left\{\mathbf{a}\right\}}\mathbf{a}^{\prime\top}\mathbf{r}^{\prime}. (29)

Furthermore, by the continuity of both terms on the right-hand side of eq. 29 , 𝐚 \mathbf{a} is strictly optimal for reward functions in some open neighborhood N N of 𝐫 ′ \mathbf{r}^{\prime} . Let N ′ ≔ N ∩ ( b , c ) | 𝒮 | N^{\prime}\coloneqq N\cap(b,c)^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} . N ′ N^{\prime} is still open in ℝ | 𝒮 | \mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} since it is the intersection of two open sets N N and ( b , c ) | 𝒮 | (b,c)^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} .

𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} must assign positive probability measure to all open sets in its support; otherwise, its support would exclude these zero-measure sets by definition E.18 . Therefore, 𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} assigns positive probability to N ′ ⊆ supp ⁡ ( 𝒟 any ) N^{\prime}\subseteq\optSupp(\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}) . ∎

###### Lemma E.21 (Expected value of similar linear functional sets) .

Let A , B ⊊ ℝ | 𝒮 | A,B\subsetneq\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} be finite, let A ′ A^{\prime} be such that ND ​ ( A ) ⊆ A ′ ⊆ A \text{{ND}}\left(A\right)\subseteq A^{\prime}\subseteq A , and let g : ℝ → ℝ g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R} be an increasing function. If B B contains a copy B ′ B^{\prime} of A ′ A^{\prime} via ϕ \phi , then 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] ≤ 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ⋅ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] . \opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]\leq\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]. (30)

If ND ​ ( B ) ∖ B ′ \text{{ND}}\left(B\right)\setminus B^{\prime} is empty, then eq. 30 is an equality. If ND ​ ( B ) ∖ B ′ \text{{ND}}\left(B\right)\setminus B^{\prime} is non-empty, g g is strictly increasing, and ∃ b < c : ( b , c ) | 𝒮 | ⊆ supp ⁡ ( 𝒟 bound ) \exists b<c\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}(b,c)^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}\subseteq\optSupp(\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}) , then eq. 30 is strict.

###### Proof.

Because g : ℝ → ℝ g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R} is increasing, it is measurable (as is max \max ). Therefore, the relevant expectations exist for all 𝒟 bound \mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}} . 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \displaystyle\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] = 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ′ ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \displaystyle=\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A^{\prime}}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] (31) = 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ⋅ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ ϕ ⋅ A ′ ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \displaystyle=\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in\phi\cdot A^{\prime}}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] (32) = 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ⋅ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ′ ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \displaystyle=\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B^{\prime}}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] (33) ≤ 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ⋅ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] . \displaystyle\leq\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi\cdot\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]. (34) Equation 31 holds because ∀ 𝐫 ∈ ℝ | 𝒮 | : max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 = max 𝐚 ∈ A ′ ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 \forall\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}=\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A^{\prime}}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r} by Lemma E.12 ’s item 2 with X ≔ A X\coloneqq A , X ′ ≔ A ′ X^{\prime}\coloneqq A^{\prime} . Equation 32 holds by Lemma E.17 . Equation 33 holds by the definition of B ′ B^{\prime} . Furthermore, our assumption on ϕ \phi guarantees that B ′ ⊆ B B^{\prime}\subseteq B . Therefore, max 𝐛 ∈ B ′ ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ≤ max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 \max_{\mathbf{b}\in B^{\prime}}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\leq\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r} , and so eq. 34 holds by the fact that g g is an increasing function. Then eq. 30 holds.

If ND ​ ( B ) ∖ B ′ \text{{ND}}\left(B\right)\setminus B^{\prime} is empty, then ND ​ ( B ) ⊆ B ′ \text{{ND}}\left(B\right)\subseteq B^{\prime} . By assumption, B ′ ⊆ B B^{\prime}\subseteq B . Then apply Lemma E.12 item 2 with X ≔ B X\coloneqq B , X ′ ≔ B ′ X^{\prime}\coloneqq B^{\prime} in order to conclude that eq. 34 is an equality. Then eq. 30 is also an equality.

Suppose that g g is strictly increasing, ND ​ ( B ) ∖ B ′ \text{{ND}}\left(B\right)\setminus B^{\prime} is non-empty, and ∃ b < c : ( b , c ) | 𝒮 | ⊆ supp ⁡ ( 𝒟 bound ) \exists b<c\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}(b,c)^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}\subseteq\optSupp(\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}) . ###### Lemma E.24 (Expectation superiority lemma) .

Let A , B ⊊ ℝ | 𝒮 | A,B\subsetneq\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} be finite and let g : ℝ → ℝ g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R} be an increasing function. In particular, 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ( max 𝐚 ∈ A 𝐚 ⊤ 𝐫 ) ] ≱ most : 𝔇 bound 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ( max 𝐛 ∈ B 𝐛 ⊤ 𝐫 ) ] \opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]\not\geq_{\text{{most}}\text{: }\mathfrak{D}_{\text{bound}}}\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] .

###### Proof.

Because g : ℝ → ℝ g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R} is increasing, it is measurable (as is max \max ). Therefore, the relevant expectations exist for all 𝒟 bound \mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}} .

Suppose that 𝒟 bound \mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}} is such that 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] < 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]<\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] . (41) Equation 38 follows by applying Lemma E.21 with permutation ϕ \phi and A ′ ≔ ND ​ ( A ) A^{\prime}\coloneqq\text{{ND}}\left(A\right) . Equation 39 follows because involutions satisfy ϕ − 1 = ϕ \phi^{-1}=\phi , and ϕ 2 \phi^{2} is therefore the identity. Equation 40 follows because we assumed that 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] < 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]<\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] . Equation 41 follows by applying Lemma E.21 with permutation ϕ \phi and and A ′ ≔ ND ​ ( A ) A^{\prime}\coloneqq\text{{ND}}\left(A\right) . By Lemma E.16 , eq. 37 holds.

Suppose g g is strictly increasing and ND ​ ( B ) ∖ B ′ \text{{ND}}\left(B\right)\setminus B^{\prime} is non-empty. Let ϕ ′ ∈ S | 𝒮 | \phi^{\prime}\in S_{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} . 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ′ ⋅ 𝒟 X ​ - iid [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \displaystyle\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{X\text{-}\textsc{iid}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] = 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 X ​ - iid [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \displaystyle=\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X\text{-}\textsc{iid}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] (42) < 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ⋅ 𝒟 X ​ - iid [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \displaystyle<\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi\cdot\mathcal{D}_{X\text{-}\textsc{iid}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] (43) = 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ′ ⋅ 𝒟 X ​ - iid [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] . \displaystyle=\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{X\text{-}\textsc{iid}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]. Therefore, ∀ ϕ ′ ∈ S | 𝒮 | : 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ′ ⋅ 𝒟 X ​ - iid [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐚 ∈ A ⁡ 𝐚 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] < 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ ϕ ′ ⋅ 𝒟 X ​ - iid [ g ⁡ ( max 𝐛 ∈ B ⁡ 𝐛 ⊤ ​ 𝐫 ) ] \forall\phi^{\prime}\in S_{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{X\text{-}\textsc{iid}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]<\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\phi^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{D}_{X\text{-}\textsc{iid}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] , and so 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ( max 𝐚 ∈ A 𝐚 ⊤ 𝐫 ) ] ≱ most : 𝔇 bound 𝔼 𝐫 ∼ 𝒟 bound [ g ( max 𝐛 ∈ B 𝐛 ⊤ 𝐫 ) ] \opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{a}\in A}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right]\not\geq_{\text{{most}}\text{: }\mathfrak{D}_{\text{bound}}}\opE_{\mathbf{r}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\text{bound}}}\left[g\left(\max_{\mathbf{b}\in B}\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{r}\right)\right] by definition 6.5 . ∎

###### Definition E.25 (Indicator function) .

Let L L be a predicate which takes input x x . 𝟙 L ⁡ ( x ) \mathbbm{1}_{L(x)} is the function which returns 1 when L ⁡ ( x ) L(x) is true, and 0 otherwise.

###### Lemma E.26 (Optimality probability inclusion relations) .

Let X , Y ⊊ ℝ | 𝒮 | X,Y\subsetneq\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} be finite and suppose Y ′ ⊆ Y Y^{\prime}\subseteq Y . p 𝒟 any ​ ( X ≥ Y ) ≤ p 𝒟 any ​ ( X ≥ Y ′ ) ≤ p 𝒟 any ​ ( X ∪ ( Y ∖ Y ′ ) ≥ Y ) . p_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(X\geq Y\right)\leq p_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(X\geq Y^{\prime}\right)\leq p_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(X\cup\left(Y\setminus Y^{\prime}\right)\geq Y\right). (45)

If ∃ b < c : ( b , c ) | 𝒮 | ⊆ supp ⁡ ( 𝒟 any ) \exists b<c\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}(b,c)^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}\subseteq\optSupp(\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}) , X ⊆ Y X\subseteq Y , and ND ​ ( Y ) ∩ ( Y ∖ Y ′ ) \text{{ND}}\left(Y\right)\cap\left(Y\setminus Y^{\prime}\right) is non-empty, then the second inequality is strict.

###### Proof.

###### Proof.

Let R R be any reward function. ### E.2 Some actions have greater probability of being optimal

###### Lemma E.33 (Optimal policy shift bound) .

For fixed R R , Π ∗ ​ ( R , γ ) \Pi^{*}\left(R,\gamma\right) can take on at most ( 2 ​ | 𝒮 | + 1 ) ​ ∑ s ( | ℱ ⁡ ( s ) | 2 ) (2\left|\mathcal{S}\right|+1)\sum_{s}\binom{\left|\Fop(s)\right|}{2} distinct values over γ ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) \gamma\in(0,1) .

###### Proof.

By Lemma E.1 , Π ∗ ​ ( R , γ ) \Pi^{*}\left(R,\gamma\right) changes value iff there is a change in optimality status for some visit distribution function at some state. Lippman [1968] showed that two visit distribution functions can trade off optimality status at most 2 ​ | 𝒮 | + 1 2\left|\mathcal{S}\right|+1 times. At each state s s , there are ( | ℱ ⁡ ( s ) | 2 ) \binom{\left|\Fop(s)\right|}{2} such pairs. ∎

###### Proposition E.34 (Optimality probability’s limits exist) .

Let F ⊆ ℱ ⁡ ( s ) F\subseteq\Fop(s) . ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , 0 ) = lim γ → 0 ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , γ ) \Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,0\right)=\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,\gamma\right) and ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , 1 ) = lim γ → 1 ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , γ ) \Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,1\right)=\lim_{\gamma\to 1}\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,\gamma\right) .

First consider the limit as γ → 1 \gamma\to 1 . Let 𝒟 any \mathcal{D}_{\text{any}} have probability measure F any F_{\text{any}} , and define δ ⁡ ( γ ) ≔ F any ​ ( { R ∈ ℝ 𝒮 ∣ ∃ γ ∗ ∈ [ γ , 1 ) : Π ∗ ​ ( R , γ ∗ ) ≠ Π ∗ ​ ( R , 1 ) } ) \delta(\gamma)\coloneqq F_{\text{any}}\left(\left\{R\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{S}}\mid\exists\gamma^{*}\in[\gamma,1)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\Pi^{*}\left(R,\gamma^{*}\right)\neq\Pi^{*}\left(R,1\right)\right\}\right) . Since F any F_{\text{any}} is a probability measure, δ ⁡ ( γ ) \delta(\gamma) is bounded [ 0 , 1 ] [0,1] , and δ ⁡ ( γ ) \delta(\gamma) is monotone decreasing. Therefore, lim γ → 1 δ ⁡ ( γ ) \lim_{\gamma\to 1}\delta(\gamma) exists.

If lim γ → 1 δ ⁡ ( γ ) > 0 \lim_{\gamma\to 1}\delta(\gamma)>0 , then there exist reward functions whose optimal policy sets Π ∗ ​ ( R , γ ) \Pi^{*}\left(R,\gamma\right) never converge (in the discrete topology on sets) to Π ∗ ​ ( R , 1 ) \Pi^{*}\left(R,1\right) , contradicting Lemma E.33 . By the definition of optimality probability ( definition 4.3 ) and of δ ⁡ ( γ ) \delta(\gamma) , | ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , γ ) − ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , 1 ) | ≤ δ ⁡ ( γ ) |\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,\gamma\right)-\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,1\right)|\leq\delta(\gamma) . Since lim γ → 1 δ ⁡ ( γ ) = 0 \lim_{\gamma\to 1}\delta(\gamma)=0 , lim γ → 1 ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , γ ) = ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , 1 ) \lim_{\gamma\to 1}\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,\gamma\right)=\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,1\right) .

A similar proof shows that lim γ → 0 ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , γ ) = ℙ 𝒟 any ( F , 0 ) \lim_{\gamma\to 0}\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,\gamma\right)=\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(F,0\right) . ∎

###### Definition E.37 (Discount-normalized value function) .

Let π \pi be a policy, R R a reward function, and s s a state. For γ ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] \gamma\in[0,1] , V R , norm π ​ ( s , γ ) ≔ lim γ ∗ → γ ( 1 − γ ∗ ) ​ V R π ​ ( s , γ ∗ ) V^{\pi}_{R,\,\text{norm}}\left(s,\gamma\right)\coloneqq\lim_{\gamma^{*}\to\gamma}(1-\gamma^{*})V^{\pi}_{R}(s,\gamma^{*}) .

###### Lemma E.38 (Normalized value functions have uniformly bounded derivative) .

There exists K ≥ 0 K\geq 0 such that for all reward functions 𝐫 ∈ ℝ | 𝒮 | \mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} , sup s ∈ 𝒮 , π ∈ Π , γ ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] | d d ​ γ ​ V R , norm π ​ ( s , γ ) | ≤ K ​ ‖ 𝐫 ‖ 1 \sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\mathcal{S},\pi\in\Pi,\gamma\in[0,1]\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{d}{d\gamma}V^{\pi}_{R,\,\text{norm}}\left(s,\gamma\right)\right|\leq K\left\lVert\mathbf{r}\right\rVert_{1} .

###### Proof.

Let π \pi be any policy, s s a state, and R R a reward function. Since V R , norm π ​ ( s , γ ) = lim γ ∗ → γ ( 1 − γ ∗ ) ​ 𝐟 π , s ​ ( γ ∗ ) ⊤ ​ 𝐫 V^{\pi}_{R,\,\text{norm}}\left(s,\gamma\right)=\lim_{\gamma^{*}\to\gamma}(1-\gamma^{*})\mathbf{f}^{\pi,s}(\gamma^{*})^{\top}\mathbf{r} , d d ​ γ ​ V R , norm π ​ ( s , γ ) \frac{d}{d\gamma}V^{\pi}_{R,\,\text{norm}}\left(s,\gamma\right) is controlled by the behavior of lim γ ∗ → γ ( 1 − γ ∗ ) ​ 𝐟 π , s ​ ( γ ∗ ) \lim_{\gamma^{*}\to\gamma}(1-\gamma^{*})\mathbf{f}^{\pi,s}(\gamma^{*}) . We show that this function’s gradient is bounded in infinity norm.

By Lemma E.4 , 𝐟 π , s ​ ( γ ) \mathbf{f}^{\pi,s}(\gamma) is a multivariate rational function on γ \gamma . ### E.4 Seeking Power is often more probable under optimality

#### E.4.1 Keeping options open tends to be Power -seeking and tends to be optimal

###### Definition E.40 (Normalized visit distribution function) .

Let 𝐟 : [ 0 , 1 ) → ℝ | 𝒮 | \mathbf{f}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}[0,1)\to\mathbb{R}^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|} be a vector function. If F F is a set of such 𝐟 \mathbf{f} , then Norm ​ ( F , γ ) ≔ { Norm ​ ( 𝐟 , γ ) ∣ 𝐟 ∈ F } \text{{Norm}}\left(F,\gamma\right)\coloneqq\left\{\text{{Norm}}\left(\mathbf{f},\gamma\right)\mid\mathbf{f}\in F\right\} .

Let Δ s ∈ Δ ⁡ ( 𝒮 ) \Delta_{s}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S}) be a state probability distribution, let π ∈ Π \pi\in\Pi , and let 𝐟 ∗ ≔ 𝔼 s ∼ Δ s [ 𝐟 π , s ] \mathbf{f}^{*}\coloneqq\opE_{s\sim\Delta_{s}}\left[\mathbf{f}^{\pi,s}\right] . (123) Equation 122 follows because the expectation is over a finite set. Therefore, each Norm ​ ( 𝐟 π , s , γ ) \text{{Norm}}\left(\mathbf{f}^{\pi,s},\gamma\right) is continuous on γ ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] \gamma\in[0,1] . Lastly, eq. 123 ’s expectation over finitely many continuous functions is itself continuous. ∎

###### Lemma E.45 (Non-dominated visit distribution functions never agree with other visit distribution functions at that state) .

Let 𝐟 ∈ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) , 𝐟 ′ ∈ ℱ ⁡ ( s ) ∖ { 𝐟 } \mathbf{f}\in\Fndop(s),\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\in\Fop(s)\setminus\{\mathbf{f}\} . ∀ γ ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) : 𝐟 ⁡ ( γ ) ≠ 𝐟 ′ ​ ( γ ) \forall\gamma\in(0,1)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbf{f}(\gamma)\neq\mathbf{f}^{\prime}(\gamma) .

###### Proof.

Let γ ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) \gamma\in(0,1) . Since 𝐟 ∈ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) \mathbf{f}\in\Fndop(s) , there exists a γ ∗ ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) \gamma^{*}\in(0,1) at which 𝐟 \mathbf{f} is strictly optimal for some reward function. Then by section D.1.2 , we can produce another reward function for which 𝐟 \mathbf{f} is strictly optimal at discount rate γ \gamma ; in particular, section D.1.2 guarantees that the policies which induce 𝐟 ′ \mathbf{f}^{\prime} are not optimal at γ \gamma . So 𝐟 ⁡ ( γ ) ≠ 𝐟 ′ ​ ( γ ) \mathbf{f}(\gamma)\neq\mathbf{f}^{\prime}(\gamma) . ∎

###### Corollary E.46 (Cardinality of non-dominated visit distributions) .

Let F ⊆ ℱ ⁡ ( s ) F\subseteq\Fop(s) . ∀ γ ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) : | F ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) | = | F ⁡ ( γ ) ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s , γ ) | \forall\gamma\in(0,1)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\left|F\cap\Fndop(s)\right|=\left|F(\gamma)\cap\Fndop(s,\gamma)\right| .

###### Proof.

Let γ ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) \gamma\in(0,1) . By applying Lemma E.31 with Δ d ≔ 𝐞 s \Delta_{d}\coloneqq\mathbf{e}_{s} , 𝐟 ∈ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) = ND ​ ( ℱ ⁡ ( s ) ) \mathbf{f}\in\Fndop(s)=\text{{ND}}\left(\Fop(s)\right) iff 𝐟 ⁡ ( γ ) ∈ ND ​ ( ℱ ⁡ ( s , γ ) ) \mathbf{f}(\gamma)\in\text{{ND}}\left(\Fop(s,\gamma)\right) . By Lemma E.32 , ND ​ ( ℱ ⁡ ( s , γ ) ) = ℱ nd ⁡ ( s , γ ) \text{{ND}}\left(\Fop(s,\gamma)\right)=\Fndop(s,\gamma) . So all 𝐟 ∈ F ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) \mathbf{f}\in F\cap\Fndop(s) induce 𝐟 ⁡ ( γ ) ∈ F ⁡ ( γ ) ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s , γ ) \mathbf{f}(\gamma)\in F(\gamma)\cap\Fndop(s,\gamma) , and | F ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) | ≥ | F ⁡ ( γ ) ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s , γ ) | \left|F\cap\Fndop(s)\right|\geq\left|F(\gamma)\cap\Fndop(s,\gamma)\right| .

Lemma E.45 implies that for all 𝐟 , 𝐟 ′ ∈ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) \mathbf{f},\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\in\Fndop(s) , 𝐟 = 𝐟 ′ \mathbf{f}=\mathbf{f}^{\prime} iff 𝐟 ​ ( γ ) = 𝐟 ′ ​ ( γ ) \mathbf{f}(\gamma)=\mathbf{f}^{\prime}(\gamma) . Therefore, | F ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) | ≤ | F ⁡ ( γ ) ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s , γ ) | \left|F\cap\Fndop(s)\right|\leq\left|F(\gamma)\cap\Fndop(s,\gamma)\right| . So | F ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s ) | = | F ⁡ ( γ ) ∩ ℱ nd ⁡ ( s , γ ) | \left|F\cap\Fndop(s)\right|=\left|F(\gamma)\cap\Fndop(s,\gamma)\right| . ∎

#### E.4.2 When γ = 1 \gamma=1 , optimal policies tend to navigate towards “larger” sets of cycles

###### Proposition E.50 ( Rsd properties) .

Let 𝐝 ∈ RSD ​ ( s ) \mathbf{d}\in\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right) . 𝐝 \mathbf{d} is element-wise non-negative and ‖ 𝐝 ‖ 1 = 1 \left\lVert\mathbf{d}\right\rVert_{1}=1 .

###### Proof.

𝐝 \mathbf{d} has non-negative elements because it equals the limit of lim γ → 1 ( 1 − γ ) ​ 𝐟 ​ ( γ ) \lim_{\gamma\to 1}(1-\gamma)\mathbf{f}(\gamma) , whose elements are non-negative by Proposition E.3 item 1 . ‖ 𝐝 ‖ 1 \displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{d}\right\rVert_{1} = ‖ lim γ → 1 ( 1 − γ ) ​ 𝐟 ​ ( γ ) ‖ 1 \displaystyle=\left\lVert\lim_{\gamma\to 1}(1-\gamma)\mathbf{f}(\gamma)\right\rVert_{1} (219) = lim γ → 1 ( 1 − γ ) ​ ‖ 𝐟 ⁡ ( γ ) ‖ 1 \displaystyle=\lim_{\gamma\to 1}(1-\gamma)\left\lVert\mathbf{f}(\gamma)\right\rVert_{1} (220) = 1 . \displaystyle=1. (221) Equation 219 follows because the definition of rsd s ( definition 6.10 ) ensures that ∃ 𝐟 ∈ ℱ ⁡ ( s ) : lim γ → 1 ( 1 − γ ) ​ 𝐟 ​ ( γ ) = 𝐝 \exists\mathbf{f}\in\Fop(s)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\lim_{\gamma\to 1}(1-\gamma)\mathbf{f}(\gamma)=\mathbf{d} . Equation 220 follows because ‖ ⋅ ‖ 1 \left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{1} is a continuous function. Equation 221 follows because ‖ 𝐟 ⁡ ( γ ) ‖ 1 = 1 1 − γ \left\lVert\mathbf{f}(\gamma)\right\rVert_{1}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma} by Proposition E.3 item 2 . ∎

###### Lemma E.51 (When reachable with probability 1, 1-cycles induce non-dominated rsd s) .

If 𝐞 s ′ ∈ RSD ​ ( s ) \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right) , then 𝐞 s ′ ∈ RSD nd ​ ( s ) \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}{\text{\textsubscript{nd}}}\left(s\right) .

###### Proof.

If 𝐝 ∈ RSD ​ ( s ) \mathbf{d}\in\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right) is distinct from 𝐞 s ′ \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}} , then ‖ 𝐝 ‖ 1 = 1 \left\lVert\mathbf{d}\right\rVert_{1}=1 and 𝐝 \mathbf{d} has non-negative entries by Proposition E.50 . Since 𝐝 \mathbf{d} is distinct from 𝐞 s ′ \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}} , then its entry for index s ′ s^{\prime} must be strictly less than 1: 𝐝 ⊤ ​ 𝐞 s ′ < 1 = 𝐞 s ′ ⊤ ​ 𝐞 s ′ \mathbf{d}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}<1=\mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}} . Therefore, 𝐞 s ′ ∈ RSD ​ ( s ) \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right) is strictly optimal for the reward function 𝐫 ≔ 𝐞 s ′ \mathbf{r}\coloneqq\mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}} , and so 𝐞 s ′ ∈ RSD nd ​ ( s ) \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}{\text{\textsubscript{nd}}}\left(s\right) . ∎

See 6.14

###### Proof.

Suppose 𝐞 s x , 𝐞 s ′ ∈ RSD ​ ( s ) \mathbf{e}_{s_{x}},\mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right) are distinct. Let ϕ ≔ ( s x ​ s ′ ) , D ′ ≔ { 𝐞 s x } , D ≔ RSD ​ ( s ) ∖ { 𝐞 s x } \phi\coloneqq(s_{x}\,\,\,s^{\prime}),D^{\prime}\coloneqq\left\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\right\},D\coloneqq\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right)\setminus\left\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\right\} . ϕ ⋅ D ′ = { 𝐞 s ′ } ⊆ RSD ​ ( s ) ∖ { 𝐞 s x } ≕ D \phi\cdot D^{\prime}=\left\{\mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}\right\}\subseteq\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right)\setminus\left\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\right\}\eqqcolon D since s x ≠ s ′ s_{x}\neq s^{\prime} . D ′ ∪ D = RSD ​ ( s ) D^{\prime}\cup D=\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right) and RSD nd ​ ( s ) ∖ ( D ′ ∪ D ) = RSD nd ​ ( s ) ∖ RSD ​ ( s ) = ∅ \text{{RSD}}{\text{\textsubscript{nd}}}\left(s\right)\setminus(D^{\prime}\cup D)=\text{{RSD}}{\text{\textsubscript{nd}}}\left(s\right)\setminus\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right)=\emptyset trivially have pairwise orthogonal vector elements. Then apply Theorem 6.13 to conclude that ℙ 𝒟 any ( { 𝐞 s x } , average ) ≤ most : 𝔇 any ℙ 𝒟 any ( RSD ( s ) ∖ { 𝐞 s x } , average ) \Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\},\text{average}\right)\leq_{\text{{most}}\text{: }\mathfrak{D}_{\text{any}}}\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right)\setminus\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\},\text{average}\right) .

Suppose there exists another 𝐞 s ′′ ∈ RSD ​ ( s ) \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right) . By Lemma E.51 , 𝐞 s ′′ ∈ RSD nd ​ ( s ) \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}{\text{\textsubscript{nd}}}\left(s\right) . Furthermore, since s ′′ ∉ { s ′ , s x } s^{\prime\prime}\not\in\left\{s^{\prime},s_{x}\right\} , 𝐞 s ′′ ∈ ( RSD ​ ( s ) ∖ { 𝐞 s x } ) ∖ { 𝐞 s ′ } = D ∖ ϕ ⋅ D ′ \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime\prime}}\in\left(\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right)\setminus\left\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\right\}\right)\setminus\left\{\mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime}}\right\}=D\setminus\phi\cdot D^{\prime} . Therefore, 𝐞 s ′′ ∈ RSD nd ​ ( s ) ∩ ( D ∖ ϕ ⋅ D ′ ) \mathbf{e}_{s^{\prime\prime}}\in\text{{RSD}}{\text{\textsubscript{nd}}}\left(s\right)\cap\left(D\setminus\phi\cdot D^{\prime}\right) . Then apply the second condition of Theorem 6.13 to conclude that ℙ 𝒟 any ( { 𝐞 s x } , average ) ≱ most : 𝔇 bound ℙ 𝒟 any ( RSD ( s ) ∖ { 𝐞 s x } , average ) \Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\},\text{average}\right)\not\geq_{\text{{most}}\text{: }\mathfrak{D}_{\text{bound}}}\Prb\nolimits_{\mathcal{D}_{\text{any}}}\left(\text{{RSD}}\left(s\right)\setminus\{\mathbf{e}_{s_{x}}\},\text{average}\right) . ∎

## Instructions for reporting errors

We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the methods listed below:

Click the "Report Issue" ( ) button, located in the page header.

Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.

Our team has already identified the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for all.

Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion , and welcome developer contributions .
